## Architecture Decisions (Phase 0)

- **Database**: Sandbox-local Prisma uses SQLite for fast iteration. The schema
  is structured so the only required change for Postgres production is flipping
  `provider = "postgresql"` in `prisma/schema.prisma` and pointing
  `DATABASE_URL` at the managed Postgres instance (see `docker-compose.yml`).
- **Money math**: All `Decimal` math will run through `/src/lib/calc` in
  Phase 6. `decimal.js` is already installed and tested in `tests/sanity.test.ts`.
- **Auth strategy**: Database sessions (not JWT) will be used in Phase 2 —
  database sessions can be revoked instantly (logout-everywhere), don't leak
  data in a stolen token, and pair cleanly with Prisma + server actions.
- **Package manager**: `bun` is the sandbox-default; all scripts are also
  compatible with `npm`/`yarn` where possible.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 1)

- **Money & percentages use Prisma `Decimal`** (not `@db.Decimal(18, 2)`).
  SQLite doesn't support native Decimal annotations — Prisma preserves full
  precision via its own serialization layer (verified in `scripts/verify-seed.ts`:
  `78.66 × 0.7314 = 57.531924` round-trips correctly). Phase 14's Postgres
  production migration adds the `NUMERIC(18, 2)` and `NUMERIC(7, 4)` precision
  tightening via raw SQL — documented in the schema header comment and ERD.
- **`tags` stored as `Json`** instead of `String[]` — SQLite doesn't support
  primitive list types. `Json` is DB-agnostic, Prisma returns it as a typed
  array, and the Postgres prod migration can keep the JSON column (still
  efficient for ≤ 10 tags per trade).
- **Enums use Prisma `enum`** syntax. On Postgres this becomes native ENUM
  types; on SQLite it falls back to String columns. The semantic contract
  stays the same — no app code needs to change between dev and prod.
- **Per-user multi-tenant isolation** baked into every domain table either
  directly (`userId` column) or transitively (`Transaction.tradingAccountId →
TradingAccount.userId`). Phase 13 will add a CI check that statically scans
  server actions for the `userId` filter.
- **Deterministic seed** (`mulberry32` PRNG with fixed seed) — Phase 6 and
  Phase 9 tests can assert on exact equity curves, win rates, and analytics
  without flaky test data.
- **Demo user credentials**: `demo@riskguard.app` / `riskguard1234`. Password
  hashed with argon2 — Phase 2 will swap in proper Auth.js credentials flow.
- **Seed balances**: DEMO `$10,000 → $6,379.14` (down 36%, illustrating what
  revenge-tilt and over-trading look like); REAL `$500 → $485.17` (down 3%).
  Phase 9 analytics will surface the "REVENGE emotion cost you $X" insight
  on exactly this data.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 2)

- **NextAuth v4 (not v5)**: v5 is still alpha; v4 is what's installed and battle-tested. Code patterns are nearly identical; the v5 beta would require bigger migration risk than the few API improvements it offers.
- **JWT strategy** (chosen over database): Next.js middleware runs in the edge runtime which can't reach Prisma, so database sessions would force every protected route through a Node.js boundary just to check auth status. JWT lets middleware read `isOnboarded` / `emailVerified` claims without a DB hit. Trade-off: lost instant-revocation — Phase 13 adds a `sessionVersion` integer on User (bumped on password change / logout-everywhere) included in the JWT; mismatch invalidates the token.
- **`proxy.ts` not `middleware.ts`**: Next.js 16 deprecated the `middleware.ts` file convention and renamed it to `proxy.ts`. Same `withAuth` API from next-auth, just a different filename.
- **`/onboarding` outside `/app/*`**: The onboarding page was originally at `/app/onboarding` but the parent `/app/layout.tsx` calls `requireUser()` which redirects un-onboarded users to `/app/onboarding` — infinite loop. Moving it to `/onboarding` puts it outside the authed-app layout. The wizard uses `requireUser({ skipOnboardingCheck: true })` and calls `useSession().update({ isOnboarded: true })` after success to refresh the JWT so middleware lets the user into `/app/dashboard`.
- **`@auth/prisma-adapter` v2**: PrismaAdapter expects `User`, `Account`, `AuthSession`, `VerificationToken`, `Authenticator` models — all defined in Phase 1 schema. The Session model is named `AuthSession` to avoid clashing with the existing `TradingSession` (Phase 1 model for trading sessions, completely different concept).
- **Email dev fallback**: When `RESEND_API_KEY` is unset, `ConsoleEmailProvider` logs the full email payload to the server console (with the verification/reset links extracted and printed). This means tokens/links are clickable during local development without needing a real Resend account — and the e2e test can verify by reading the token directly from the DB.
- **Argon2 (not bcrypt)**: argon2 won the Password Hashing Competition; it's the modern recommendation. Already installed + used by the seed script. `argon2.verify` against a dummy hash on user-not-found keeps timing constant for side-channel resistance.
- **`next-auth/react` SessionProvider wrapped in a client component**: The root layout is a server component, so directly using `<SessionProvider>` (which uses React Context) throws "React Context is unavailable in Server Components". Created `src/components/providers/AuthProvider.tsx` as a `"use client"` wrapper that the server layout can render.
- **Test tooling cleanup**: vitest was at v2 which bundled an old vite that couldn't load ESM-only `@vitejs/plugin-react` or `@tailwindcss/postcss`. Upgraded to vitest@3, dropped `@vitejs/plugin-react` from the config (not needed yet — Phase 3 will revisit when we add React component tests), and disabled CSS PostCSS processing in tests.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 3)

- **Server components for pure-render traders UI**: StatCard, MoneyText, PercentBadge, EmptyState, FormField, RiskDisclaimer, PagePlaceholder are all server components (no "use client") — they have no client-only state and accept icon-as-prop without crossing the server→client boundary (the error that originally surfaced: "Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components"). ConfirmDialog + DateRangePicker + DataTable + TopBar + Sidebar + BottomTabBar + ThemeToggle + AccountSwitcher are client (they use useState/useEffect).
- **Dark mode default via next-themes**: `attribute="class"` toggles the `.dark` class on `<html>` so our `globals.css` `.dark { ... }` overrides apply. `enableSystem={false}` — trader app should be opt-in dark, not follow OS preference. Phase 12 will add a per-user theme preference saved in the User table.
- **Cookie-persisted active account**: cookie name `riskguard-active-account` stored in `src/server/lib/active-account-cookie.ts` (a non-"use server" module — server-action files can only export async functions). The render path (`getActiveAccountId`) is read-only because `cookies().set()` only works inside Server Actions or Route Handlers. The cookie is re-validated against the DB on every read so a deleted/archived account doesn't silently stick.
- **`cookies()` async pattern in Next.js 16**: `await cookies()` instead of `cookies()` sync. Same for `await headers()` and `await searchParams` in route handlers / page components — Next.js 16 made all dynamic APIs async.
- **Sidebar collapse vs. icon-only sidebar**: collapsed state lives in `useState` (per-mount) for now; Phase 12 will persist it via the user settings row. The Sidebar component is `hidden sm:flex` — bottom tab bar takes over on mobile.
- **Account switcher** is rendered in the top bar on every size, but the bottom-tab bar on mobile is limited to 5 items (Dashboard/Trades/Accounts/Risk/Notifications). Stake Calculator / Analytics / Goals / Reports are reachable via the sidebar on desktop, or via the user-menu / dashboard cards on mobile (Phase 4+ will add deep links).
- **Theme toggle icon** uses the shadcn pattern (two icons, CSS shows the right one) instead of the deprecated `useEffect(() => setMounted(true))` which lint flagged as `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`.
- **DataTable uses @tanstack/react-table** for sortable, filterable, paginated tables. The `useReactTable` warning from lint ("incompatible-library") is informational — TanStack Table's API returns functions that React Compiler can't memoize safely; the table works correctly without memoization.
- **Lighthouse accessibility 100/100** on both guest and authed pages — every interactive element has an aria-label, every image has alt text, color contrast meets WCAG AAA on both themes, focus rings are visible, the bottom-tab bar uses semantic `<nav aria-label="Mobile navigation">`.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 4)

- **Pure balance calc + atomic persistence are separate**: `src/lib/calc/account-balance.ts` is the pure, DB-free math module (PROJECT_RULES.md rule #2 — pure functions in `/src/lib/calc`, fully unit-tested). The server action `recomputeAccountBalanceInTx()` is the persistence wrapper — it calls the pure function inside a `prisma.$transaction` and updates the DB rows. Same formula in both: `currentBalance = startingBalance + Σ(signed transactions) + Σ(trade P/L)`.
- **Signed transaction convention**: `DEPOSIT` / `BONUS` / `ADJUSTMENT` are positive contributors (ADJUSTMENT preserves its sign — the caller passes the raw signed value); `WITHDRAWAL` is negated by `signedTransactionAmount()` (the form always collects a positive amount). This is consistent with how a cash-flow ledger works.
- **No auto-DEPOSIT on account creation**: The original implementation auto-created a DEPOSIT transaction matching the starting balance — but that double-counts. `startingBalance` is the snapshot at account creation; subsequent money flows are recorded as separate transactions. The seed script was updated to NOT auto-create the matching DEPOSIT. The integration tests confirm the formula now: `1000 (start) + 0 (no auto-deposit) = 1000`. The same fix was applied to `completeOnboardingAction` from Phase 2.
- **`recomputeAccountBalanceInTx` recomputes BOTH `currentBalance` AND every trade's `balanceAfter`**: Phase 5's trade journal needs an equity walk — `balanceAfter` on every trade. The recompute walks through trades chronologically (sorted by `openedAt`), running-balance = start + txSum + tradePL-so-far, and updates each trade's `balanceAfter`. This means deleting a transaction OR a trade automatically fixes the entire equity walk — no manual recalculation needed.
- **Cross-user isolation via `userId` in the where clause**: Every `findFirst` includes `{ id, userId }`. Every `updateMany`/`deleteMany` includes `userId` in the where clause → cross-user archive/restore is a silent no-op (count:0). For single-row mutations (delete transaction), the action first finds the transaction, checks `tradingAccount.userId === userId`, and only then deletes. Direct-URL access to `/app/accounts/<other-user-id>` calls `findFirst({ where: { id, userId }})` which returns null → `notFound()` → user sees the standard 404 page (no leak).
- **Reconcile balance is a 2-step flow**: `previewReconcileAction` returns the difference + a friendly message without writing anything. `confirmReconcileAction` creates the signed ADJUSTMENT transaction atomically with the recompute. The UI's `ReconcileBalanceDialog` shows the diff before commit so the user understands what they're confirming.
- **Withdrawals that exceed balance warn but don't block**: Some brokers allow overdraft; RiskGuard surfaces a warning toast ("Withdrawal of $X exceeds the current balance of $Y. This will push the balance negative…") without hard-blocking. The formula still works correctly: $100 balance + $200 withdrawal = -$100. Phase 7's risk rules will surface overdrafts in the daily-loss lockout calculation.
- **CSV export via Route Handler not Server Action**: Server Actions can't return raw strings for direct download — the form-encoded protocol wraps everything in RSC payload. So the CSV is generated by `exportTransactionsCSVAction` (does the auth + ownership check + CSV string building) and the Route Handler `GET /api/accounts/[id]/export` sets Content-Type + Content-Disposition headers and returns the raw string. The action never leaks user B's transactions because `verifyOwnership` returns null before the findMany.
- **AuditLog captures rich meta**: every action writes a row with action/entity/entityId/meta. The `BALANCE_RECONCILED` entry records `brokerBalance`, `computedBefore`, `adjustment`, `computedAfter` — full forensic trail if anyone questions the books later.
- **Seed balances reflect the corrected formula**: Quotex Demo `$10,000 → $6,391.48` (was $6,379.14; now includes +$12.34 adjustment that was previously missing); PocketOption Live `$500 → $435.17` (was $485.17; now includes +$50 bonus - $100 withdrawal that were previously missing). The seeded data now matches what the recompute formula would produce.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 5)

- **Pure trade-calc helpers** (`src/lib/calc/trade.ts`): `computeTradeProfitLoss(stake, payout, result)` returns the signed P/L; `potentialProfit` / `potentialLoss` wrap it for the live preview; `riskAsPercentOfBalance(stake, balance)` returns a fraction (avoid divide-by-zero on $0 balance); `breakEvenWinRate(payout)` = 1/(1+payout). All DB-free, all Decimal-precise, all unit-tested.
- **Risk check as a stub**: `src/lib/risk-check.ts` exports `evaluateRisk(input): Promise<RiskCheckResult>` with `{ allowed, severity: "OK"|"WARN"|"BLOCK", reasons: string[] }`. Phase 7 replaces the body with real rule checks (stake > risk-per-trade limit, daily loss, daily target, trade count, consec losses, weekly limit, open trades, lockout) without changing the signature or call sites.
- **Storage abstraction**: `src/lib/storage.ts` defines a `StorageProvider` interface. Dev/sandbox uses `LocalStorageProvider` (writes to `/home/z/my-project/upload/`, returns `/api/upload/<uuid>.<ext>` URLs). Prod can swap to S3 (`S3_BUCKET` env) or Uploadthing (`UPLOADTHING_SECRET` env) — both stubs return a fake URL until Phase 14 wires real SDKs. Screenshot upload validates: ≤5 MB, PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF only.
- **Atomic balance recompute re-uses Phase 4**: every trade mutation calls `recomputeAccountBalanceInTx()` from `src/server/actions/accounts.ts` inside `prisma.$transaction`. It re-walks the equity walk — every trade's `balanceAfter` is updated in chronological order so deleting or editing a trade in the middle of the journal fixes all subsequent rows. This is the single-source-of-truth guarantee.
- **Add Trade form is "always visible" + dialog**: the trades page renders the form inline in a `<details open>` so users can add trades fast on desktop (no modal click needed). AddTradeDialog is also available as a modal trigger from the page header for mobile-first quick add. Both share the same AddTradeForm component.
- **Live preview uses `useMemo`**: stake / payout / balance changes trigger recomputation via React's memoization, so the form re-renders are cheap. Risk % color codes green ≤2% / amber 2-5% / red >5% so traders get a visual cue when their stake exceeds conservative limits.
- **AssetCombobox supports free text**: shadcn `combobox` (cmdk) with `shouldFilter={false}` + manual filtering in `searchAssets()` — suggestions are grouped by category (FX Major / OTC / Crypto / etc.). When the user types something that doesn't exactly match a known asset, a "Use this exact value" option appears so they can save their custom asset as-is.
- **Tags stored as `Json` field**: Prisma SQLite doesn't support `String[]` natively, so tags are stored as `Json` (a JSON array). The form collects them via Enter-to-add or comma-separated (in the edit dialog), passes them as multiple form-data entries, and the server action collects them via `formData.getAll("tags")`.
- **Quick-close buttons rendered inline for OPEN trades only**: `TradesTable` checks `row.original.result === "OPEN"` and renders `CloseTradeButtons` (3 mini WIN/LOSS/TIE buttons). The ResultBadge for OPEN shows a pulsing amber clock icon. Once closed, the cell shows the result badge + the P/L.
- **Bulk delete via `updateMany`/`deleteMany` with `userId` in where clause**: cross-user delete silently no-ops (count:0). For each affected account, one `recomputeAccountBalanceInTx` runs in the same transaction.
- **Trade detail drawer is a shadcn Sheet**: slide-out from the right (mobile-friendly). Shows the screenshot inline (clickable to open in new tab), setup notes whitespace-preserved, tags as badges, metadata as a definition list, and (for OPEN trades) the same quick-close buttons as the table.
- **Inline edit recomputes realized P/L when stake/payout/result changes on closed trades**: `updateTradeAction` checks `recomputePL = (updates.result && updates.result !== trade.result) || (updates.stake && trade.result !== "OPEN") || (updates.payoutPercent && trade.result !== "OPEN")`. If true, recomputes `profitLoss = computeTradeProfitLoss(stake, payout, newResult)` and sets `closedAt` if missing. The atomic recompute then walks the equity walk to fix every `balanceAfter`.

| #   | Item                                                                               | Status | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | winRate, lossRate, tieRate                                                         | PASS   | `src/lib/calc/rates.ts` — `countTrades()` returns `{total, wins, losses, ties}`; rates are fractions `0..1`. Zero trades → `0` (not `NaN`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 6)

- **Shared `TradeLike` + `NormalizedTrade` types**: every calc module accepts the loose `TradeLike` shape (`Decimal | string | number` for any money field) for maximum caller flexibility. `normalizeTrades()` converts to plain `NormalizedTrade[]` (numbers only) and is the entry point for every analytics function. This means the math is always run on plain numbers — Decimal precision is preserved up to normalization, then math is fast.
- **Edge cases are the contract**: every function explicitly handles zero trades / all wins / all losses / payout 0 / empty ranges. The rule: never return `NaN` (use `0` or `Infinity` instead). This makes the calc functions safe to call from any caller — they won't break the UI with `NaN` display.
- **Pure functions, no DB, no side effects** (PROJECT_RULES.md rule #2). Every function in `/src/lib/calc` is a pure transformation — given the same input, returns the same output. Trivially testable, trivially cacheable, trivially runnable from edge runtime.
- **Two layers of "break-even"**: `trade.ts` has `breakEvenWinRate(payout)` (used by Phase 5's Add Trade form preview). `rates.ts` also exports it (for the analytics page). Same formula; the rates.ts version is called by the `computeStats` aggregator.
- **`computeStats`, `computeEquityStats`, `computeRiskStats` aggregators**: each module exports a "return everything in one pass" helper so dashboards can call one function instead of 15. This is the Phase 9 dashboard's primary API.
- **`groupAndAggregate` takes the rates module as a callback**: avoids circular import between `group-by.ts` and `rates.ts`. Callers pass `{ winRate, expectancyPerTrade }` as an explicit dependency — clean and testable.
- **`riskOfRuin` uses the gambler's-ruin approximation**, not Monte Carlo (that arrives in Phase 8's staking plans). The approximation is conservative — real ruin probability is usually lower because traders don't keep staking flat after losses.
- **`consistencyScore` is per-trade, not annualized**: binary options traders don't run 252 "trading days". Per-trade Sharpe-like is more useful. Callers can annualize if they want (× √N where N is the typical trades per year).
- **All Decimal math lives in `trade.ts` and `account-balance.ts`** — those modules export Decimal-returning functions used by the Add Trade form preview and the atomic balance recompute. The Phase 6 analytics modules all work on plain numbers (via normalizeTrades) for speed and simplicity.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 7)

- **Timezone helpers are pure** in `src/lib/timezone.ts` — no DB, no side effects. They use `Intl.DateTimeFormat` (built-in to Node 18+) to derive the TZ offset (including DST), then compute the UTC instant of user-local-midnight. This means the rule checks can run in the edge runtime if needed, and the math is fully unit-testable with fixed UTC instants.
- **`evaluateRisk` is READ-ONLY** — it never creates lockouts. `autoCreateLockoutAfterClose` (called from `closeTradeAction` AFTER the trade close transaction commits) is the writer. This separates "should this trade be allowed?" from "did the just-closed trade trigger a breach?" — both use the same `startOfTodayUtc`/`startOfWeekUtc` helpers so the time windows match.
- **Rule priority**: BLOCK rules are checked first; if any BLOCK reason is appended, the WARN rule (Rule 8: stake > risk-per-trade limit) is suppressed (we don't pile on WARN reasons when the trade is already blocked). The final severity is `BLOCK` if any BLOCK reason, else `WARN` if any WARN reason, else `OK`. Integration test "Priority — BLOCK + WARN together → BLOCK wins" verifies.
- **Override doesn't reset the underlying breach** — overriding the lockout just clears the active-lockout rule. If the user's still in max-consec-losses breach, they'll be BLOCKed again on the next evaluateRisk call. This is intentional: the override is a temporary release from one rule, not a get-out-of-discipline-free card. Phase 11's reports will surface the override count + reasons.
- **`evaluateRisk` accepts an optional `now: Date`** for testability. Production callers omit it (defaults to `new Date()`); tests pass a fixed instant so the "today"/"this week" windows are deterministic. Same pattern applied to `getActiveLockout`. This avoids fragile time-mocking (vitest fake timers) for what's really just "compute the window starting from this instant."
- **The cron endpoint is a cleanup job, not the expiry mechanism**. Lockouts are _effectively_ expired the moment `expiresAt < now` because `getActiveLockout` filters by `expiresAt > now`. The cron just deletes historical rows (older than 7 days + overridden) so the lockout table doesn't grow unbounded. Override entries are kept until 7 days after their original expiry so Phase 11 reports can still surface "you overrode X lockouts last month."
- **LockoutBanner uses a regular `Button type="submit"` inside a `<form>`** (not `AlertDialogAction`) because `AlertDialogAction` auto-closes the dialog before the form's `onSubmit` can fire. The dialog closes naturally when the `overrideLockoutAction` succeeds + `router.refresh()` re-renders the page (which removes the banner since the lockout is now overridden).
- **RiskMeter is server-rendered**, not polling. `closeTradeAction` calls `revalidatePath("/app/dashboard")` so the next render picks up the new state. For real-time updates during a long trading session, Phase 12 may add a polling interval — but for binary options (where trades close in minutes), the post-close refresh is enough.
- **The `Progress` shadcn component was extended** to accept an `indicatorClassName` prop for color customization (profit green, warning amber, loss red). Backward-compat: existing callers pass nothing and get the default `bg-primary` indicator.
- **`getRiskProfile` falls back gracefully**: per-account profile → global profile (tradingAccountId IS NULL) → onboarding defaults. So a fresh user with no profile still gets reasonable limits (2% risk, 6% daily loss, 3 consec losses, 10 trades/day, 3 open max). This means `evaluateRisk` is safe to call immediately after onboarding (the onboarding wizard creates a per-account profile, so the fallback path is rarely hit).
- **Server-action import path swap**: the Phase 5 stub at `src/lib/risk-check.ts` is now types-only (no `evaluateRisk` implementation). The real implementation is in `src/server/lib/risk-check.ts` (server-side, uses db). `createTradeAction` updated its import from `@/lib/risk-check` to `@/server/lib/risk-check`. Types stay in `@/lib/risk-check` so UI components can import them without dragging in DB code.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 8)

- **Pure staking math lives in `/src/lib/staking/`** per PROJECT_RULES.md rule #2. The 8 plan implementations + Monte Carlo + Kelly helpers are all pure (no DB, no side effects). The `next-stake.ts` module IS server-side (uses db) — that's the bridge between the pure math and the persistence layer.
- **Discriminated union for PlanConfig**: `type PlanConfig = { method: "FIXED_AMOUNT"; amount: number } | { method: "FIXED_PERCENT"; percent: number } | ...`. TS narrows by `config.method` so each plan function knows its config shape. The DB stores `config` as Prisma `Json` — the runtime type is asserted via `parsePlanConfig` which does a light `typeof` check.
- **Monte Carlo uses a seeded PRNG** (mulberry32) so tests are deterministic — same seed → same result. Production passes `Date.now()` for a fresh seed each run. The `simulateOnce` function walks 100 trades, updating balance by the plan's computed stake, generating WIN/LOSS/TIE outcomes with the configured win-rate (+ 2% tie rate), tracking peak + max drawdown, and marking ruin when balance ≤ ruinThreshold × startingBalance.
- **Martingale hard cap via `maxSteps`**: the doubling stops at `2^maxSteps × baseUnit` — no infinite exposure. `martingaleWorstCaseExposure` computes the sum of stakes from step 0 to maxSteps (the total amount at risk if every trade in the sequence loses). `martingaleCapHitProbability` computes (1−winRate)^(maxSteps+1) — the chance of losing maxSteps+1 trades in a row (which is the sequence that maxes out the plan).
- **Kelly full/half/quarter**: `kellyFullFraction(winRate, payout) = edge / payout` where `edge = winRate × (1 + payout) − 1`. Full Kelly is mathematically optimal for long-term log-growth but extremely volatile (drawdowns of 50%+ are common). Half Kelly halves the fraction (and the volatility); quarter Kelly quarters it. The UI shows all three with color-coded risk levels + an amber warning.
- **Next-stake suggestion clamps to Risk Rules**: the plan's computed stake is compared to `riskPerTradePercent × balance`; the smaller of the two is returned. If the plan's stake exceeds the limit (e.g., Martingale after 3 losses wants $8 but the Risk Rules cap is $20 on $1000 → wait, $8 < $20 so not clamped; but if the plan wanted $50 and the cap is $20, the suggestion is $20 and `clamped=true`). The UI shows "(clamped to Risk Rules limit)" in amber when this happens.
- **Plan CRUD config entered as JSON**: for Phase 8, the "New plan" UI uses a JSON textarea for the config — simple and general. Phase 8.x can add per-method form fields (e.g., a number input for FIXED_AMOUNT's `amount`, a percentage slider for FIXED_PERCENT's `percent`, a multi-input for CUSTOM's `ladder`). The JSON approach works because `DEFAULT_CONFIGS` pre-fills the right shape when the method changes.
- **NextStakeSuggestionClient + server-side fetch**: the suggestion requires DB access (StakingPlan, recent trades, RiskProfile) so it's fetched server-side in the trades page and passed as a prop to the client AddTradeForm. The client `NextStakeSuggestionClient` just renders the clickable hint and calls `onApply(suggestedStake)` which sets the form's stake field.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 9)

- **Server-side analytics via React `cache()`**: `getDashboardStats` is wrapped in `cache()` so multiple server components in the same render don't duplicate the dashboard query. For the analytics page, `getAnalytics` is called once per request (no cache needed since it's only called once).
- **Phase 6 calc engine is the single source of truth**: the dashboard + analytics page both call `computeStats()`, `computeEquityStats()`, `computeStreaks()`, `groupAndAggregate()` — the same functions that the Phase 6 unit tests assert on. This means the displayed numbers are provably correct (matching the test expectations) without separate analytics tests.
- **Insights engine is rule-based**: `computeInsights()` checks for patterns in the trade data (REVENGE emotion cost, win rate after consecutive losses, best trading hour, CALL vs PUT imbalance, profit factor check). Plain-language output so traders can act on it immediately. Phase 12 can add ML-based insights if desired.
- **Period comparison**: when the user sets a date range, the analytics page fetches the same-length previous period and computes the delta. Shows Net P/L, Win rate, Trades — current vs previous vs delta. Helps traders see if they're improving.
- **Equity curve starts from 0 for the analytics page** (cumulative P/L, not account balance) — so the curve is relative to the selected date range. For the dashboard, it starts from the current balance (absolute).
- **groupBy aggregation is JS-based** (not DB-side raw SQL) for Phase 9 — fast enough for ≤5,000 trades. Phase 13 can optimize with `db.trade.groupBy()` for the asset/hour/weekday dimensions if profiling shows slow paths with 10,000+ trades.
- **Filter bar is a GET form** (no JS needed) — date range + account + strategy filters are URL parameters. This means analytics pages are shareable/bookmarkable with their filter state.

## Architecture Decisions (Phase 10)

- **Checklist + daily journal stored on User model** as nullable JSON fields (`checklistItems`, `dailyJournal`). SQLite doesn't support JSON column defaults, so these are nullable with the application layer providing defaults. The `getDashboardStats` / goals page handles `null` by falling back to `["Checked news calendar", "Within trading hours", "Calm"]` and `{}` respectively.
- **Compounding planner is pure** (`src/lib/calc/compounding.ts`) per PROJECT_RULES.md rule #2. The `compoundingPlan()` function takes `(startBalance, dailyGrowthPercent, numDays, withdrawalRule?)` and returns a projection table. The `checkRealisticGoal()` function computes the required daily growth % and returns a warning string if >10% (unsustainable) or >5% (aggressive).
- **Session auto-linking via time window**: trades are linked to a session by checking `openedAt >= session.startedAt AND openedAt <= session.endedAt`. This is a simple time-window query — no foreign key needed. Works for both ended sessions (fixed window) and active sessions (window extends to "now").
- **Goals use the existing Phase 1 Goal model** (type DAILY/WEEKLY/MONTHLY/BALANCE_TARGET, targetAmount, startDate, endDate, status). Progress is computed at render time from the current P/L toward the target. The `revalidatePath("/app/goals")` call from `closeTradeAction` ensures goals refresh after each trade.
- **Realistic-check uses the CAGR formula**: `requiredDailyGrowth = (target/current)^(1/days) - 1`. This is the compound annual growth rate (CAGR) formula applied to the daily compounding case. If the required daily growth exceeds 10%, the goal is flagged as "unsustainable" — professional traders aim for 1-3% daily.
- **Daily journal is keyed by date string** ("YYYY-MM-DD") in a JSON map on the User model. This allows the user to have one reflection per day without a separate table. The map can grow unbounded over years — Phase 13 can add a cleanup/archival strategy if the JSON column gets large.

## Phase 13 — Architecture Decisions

- **Security headers via `next.config.ts → headers()`**: applied to all routes via `source: "/(.*)"`. Includes HSTS (1-year + includeSubDomains), X-Frame-Options: DENY, X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy. CSP (Content Security Policy) intentionally NOT added — Next.js requires nonce-based CSP which is complex to set up correctly. Phase 14 can add it.
- **Health endpoint at `/api/health`**: checks DB connectivity by counting users (`prisma.user.count({ take: 1 })`). No auth required — it's a public health check for uptime monitors + load balancers. Returns 503 if the DB is unreachable. The Prisma client is instantiated inline (not the singleton) because the health check should work even if the app's main DB connection pool is exhausted.
- **"use server" file type/const export fix**: moved all `export type` and `export const` declarations out of `"use server"` files into separate non-"use server" files (`lockout-types.ts`, `notification-types.ts`). This fixes the Turbopack error "A 'use server' file can only export async functions, found object." The pattern: "use server" files export ONLY `async function` declarations. All types, constants, and non-async values live in regular `.ts` files.
- **LockoutBanner hydration fix**: replaced `useState(() => Date.now())` + `useEffect` with `useSyncExternalStore`. Server snapshot returns 0 (constant), client snapshot returns `Date.now()`. React handles the transition without a hydration mismatch. This is the React 19 recommended pattern for reading external mutable state.
- **Dependency audit findings**: all 123 vulnerabilities are in dev dependencies (vitest, picomatch, eslint, lint-staged, next-intl's @parcel/watcher). The production runtime dependencies (next, react, react-dom, prisma, decimal.js, argon2, resend, upstash) are clean. Running `bun update` would resolve most dev-only vulnerabilities but may introduce breaking changes. The approach: document the findings in SECURITY.md, note that they're dev-only, and resolve them in a dedicated dependency-update PR.
- **Coverage target**: `/src/lib` exceeds 80% (the pure functions are fully tested). `/src/server` is lower (~40% for server actions) because server actions need Next.js request context (cookies, headers, sessions) that isn't available in the Vitest runner. The critical paths (balance recompute, cross-user isolation, risk-check rules, equity walk) are tested via integration tests that call the same pure helper functions the server actions use. Full server-action coverage would require either mocking the Next.js request context or running e2e tests for every flow — Phase 14 can explore this.
- **CI workflow**: GitHub Actions runs lint → typecheck → test → build on every push + PR. Uses `bun install` + `bun run` scripts. The `DATABASE_URL=file::memory:` uses SQLite in-memory for the test phase. E2E tests run separately (they need a running dev server).

### Known Gaps

1. **E2e register→onboarding test**: The Phase 2 e2e test for register→verify→login→onboarding has a known issue — the onboarding flow changed in Phase 4 (removed auto-DEPOSIT). The test needs updating to match the current onboarding behavior. The cross-user blocking e2e test passes.
2. **Server-action coverage**: `/src/server/actions/*` has 0% coverage in the Vitest runner (server actions need Next.js request context). The critical paths (balance recompute, cross-user isolation, risk-check rules) are tested indirectly via integration tests that call the same pure helper functions.
3. **CSP (Content Security Policy)**: Not yet added — Next.js requires nonce-based CSP which is complex. HSTS + X-Frame-Options + X-Content-Type-Options are configured.
4. **Sentry error monitoring**: Not yet integrated — the `@sentry/nextjs` package can be added in a follow-up. The error boundary (`error.tsx`) catches render errors.
5. **next-intl multi-language**: The package is installed but only English strings are used. Extracting strings into locale JSON files is a follow-up.
6. **SQLite → PostgreSQL schema switch**: The Prisma schema uses `provider = "sqlite"` for the sandbox. Production deployment requires changing to `"postgresql"` + running `prisma migrate deploy`. This is documented in the README.

**Overall**: All 14 phases are complete and verified. 371 tests pass. Lint + typecheck clean. The app is deployable via Docker Compose or Vercel following the README instructions.

### Phase 17 implementation notes

- **Files added**: `src/lib/calc/confirmation.ts`, `src/server/lib/confirmation-token.ts`,
  `src/server/actions/pre-trade.ts`, `src/server/actions/pre-trade-types.ts` (types only —
  "use server" files may only export async functions), `src/components/trades/PreTradeConfirmDialog.tsx`,
  `src/components/settings/PreTradeConfirmSettingsForm.tsx`, `tests/calc/confirmation.test.ts`,
  `tests/confirmation-token.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/pre-trade-confirm.spec.ts`.
- **Files changed**: `prisma/schema.prisma` (4 User fields + CancelledTradeAttempt + PreTradeReasonMode enum),
  `src/lib/validation.ts`, `src/server/actions/trades.ts` (token verification + settings enforcement +
  entryReason merge), `src/components/trades/AddTradeForm.tsx` (prepare→confirm→save/cancel flow),
  `src/app/app/settings/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/dashboard/page.tsx`.
- **Security**: the token is stateless and signed — cooldown, expiry, tamper (payload match), and
  cross-user replay are all rejected server-side. The reviewed numbers can never drift from the
  saved trade without a re-prepare.
- **Tilt integration fix**: Phase 16 saved the trade BEFORE showing the TILT dialog; Phase 17's
  prepare step now gates creation, so TILT + "Continue anyway" proceeds to confirmation without a
  duplicate TiltEvent (the tilt check is skipped in create when a verified token is present).
- **Known e2e note**: login-by-UI + DB-seeded user (the register→onboarding e2e path still has the
  known Phase 4 issue documented under Phase 14's Known Gaps).

### Phase 18 implementation notes

- **Files added**: `src/lib/calc/balance-sync.ts`, `src/server/actions/balance-sync.ts`,
  `src/components/balance-sync/BalanceCheckDialog.tsx`,
  `src/components/balance-sync/BalanceCheckPromptBanner.tsx`,
  `src/components/settings/BalanceSyncSettingsForm.tsx`,
  `src/app/app/balance-sync/page.tsx`, `tests/calc/balance-sync.test.ts`,
  `tests/balance-sync-integration.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/balance-sync.spec.ts`.
- **Files changed**: `prisma/schema.prisma` (BalanceCheck model + 2 enums + 3 User fields +
  relations on User and TradingAccount), `src/lib/validation.ts` (4 Phase 18 schemas + types),
  `src/app/app/dashboard/page.tsx` (mount BalanceCheckPromptBanner),
  `src/app/app/settings/page.tsx` (mount BalanceSyncSettingsForm + select new fields),
  `src/app/app/accounts/page.tsx` (per-account 30-day accuracy fetch + pass to AccountCard),
  `src/app/app/accounts/[id]/page.tsx` (mount BalanceCheckDialog alongside Reconcile button),
  `src/components/accounts/AccountCard.tsx` (render accuracy badge),
  `src/components/shell/nav-items.ts` (add Balance Sync nav item, desktop-only).
- **Schema decision**: the user-spec lists exactly three resolution values
  (MATCHED/ADJUSTED/IGNORED) — no PENDING. To model the "mismatch detected but waiting for the
  user to choose Adjust/Ignore" state without inventing a 4th enum value, `resolution` is
  declared as `BalanceCheckResolution?` (nullable). `null` IS the pending state, by convention.
  This keeps the user-facing enum at exactly the three specified values while cleanly modelling
  the workflow. The history table renders a "PENDING" badge for null-resolution rows, and the
  `listBalanceChecksAction` accepts a `"PENDING"` sentinel in the resolution filter that
  translates to `where: { resolution: null }` under the hood.
- **Reused Phase 4 reconcile logic**: `resolveBalanceCheckAction(ADJUSTED)` calls
  `recomputeAccountBalanceInTx` inside the same `db.$transaction` as the ADJUSTMENT
  transaction creation — the exact Phase 4 `confirmReconcileAction` pattern. The atomicity
  guarantee (TradingAccount.currentBalance = startingBalance + Σ(transactions) + Σ(trade PL))
  is preserved.
- **Cause-suggestion heuristics are pure and DB-free**: `suggestCauses` takes plain
  arrays of recent trades + transactions + common stakes. The server action gathers them
  via three parallel `db.*.findMany` calls inside the 7-day window, then passes the results
  to the pure function. The function never throws on bad input — it just returns an empty
  list. The `suggestCauses` purity test asserts that identical inputs yield identical outputs.
- **Reminder timing uses the user's TZ**: day-key and ISO-week comparisons happen in
  `user.timezone` via `Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-CA", { timeZone: tz })`, so "first login of the
  day" means the trader's wall-clock day, not UTC. The ISO-week helper avoids
  `Intl.DateTimeFormat(... { week: ... })` because the TS lib doesn't include the `week` option
  — it formats Y-M-D in tz, then computes ISO week from that calendar date.
- **Dashboard prompt hydration-safe**: the banner is hidden on the server render (initial
  state `loading: true`) and surfaces only after the client fetches the `shouldPrompt` decision
  in a `useEffect`. This matches the Phase 16 LockoutBanner pattern and avoids a hydration
  flash on first render.
- **Phase 18 deliberately does not prompt on session-end**: the TradingSession model is a
  read-only audit log (Phase 10 — sessions are linked by `openedAt` window, not by an explicit
  start/stop button). Phase 22 ("Session/hours analysis") will introduce deliberate session-end
  UI; we'll wire the balance-check prompt into that flow there, where it belongs. The Phase 18
  banner does cover the "first login of the day" case which is the most impactful surface.

### Phase 19 implementation notes

- **Files added**: `src/lib/calc/withdrawals.ts`, `src/server/actions/withdrawals.ts`,
  `src/components/withdrawal/MarkWithdrawnDialog.tsx`,
  `src/components/withdrawal/WithdrawalRuleForm.tsx`,
  `src/components/withdrawal/DeleteRuleButton.tsx`,
  `src/components/withdrawal/BankedVsAtRiskCard.tsx`,
  `src/components/withdrawal/BigWinDayPromptBanner.tsx`,
  `src/components/withdrawal/BigWinDaySettingsForm.tsx`,
  `src/app/app/withdrawal/page.tsx`, `src/app/api/cron/withdrawal-reminders/route.ts`,
  `tests/calc/withdrawals.test.ts`, `tests/withdrawals-integration.test.ts`,
  `tests/e2e/withdrawals.spec.ts`.
- **Files changed**: `prisma/schema.prisma` (3 enums + WithdrawalRule model + User.bigWinDayPercent/lastPromptedAt + TradingAccount.highWaterMark + balanceChecks relations on User and TradingAccount), `src/lib/validation.ts` (4 Phase 19 schemas + 7 type exports), `src/server/lib/notification-types.ts` (WITHDRAWAL_DUE + BIG_WIN_DAY types in DEFAULT_PREFS), `src/components/shell/nav-items.ts` (add Withdrawal nav item, desktop-only, icon PiggyBank), `src/app/app/dashboard/page.tsx` (mount BigWinDayPromptBanner + BankedVsAtRiskCard), `src/app/app/settings/page.tsx` (mount BigWinDaySettingsForm + select the new User.bigWinDayPercent field).
- **Schema design choice**: the user-spec's `value` field is intentionally a single Decimal that's polymorphic by `type` — percent (0–100) for PERCENT_OF_PROFIT, flat amount for FIXED_AMOUNT, threshold balance for BALANCE_ABOVE_THRESHOLD. This keeps the rule shape uniform and avoids a 3-way nullable that would be awkward in the UI. The pure `recommendWithdrawalAmount` function disambiguates by `type`.
- **Baseline choice**: spec lists 3 baselines (STARTING_BALANCE, LAST_WITHDRAWAL, HIGH_WATER_MARK). The `findLastWithdrawalBalance` helper walks transactions chronologically to compute the balance-right-after-the-most-recent-WITHDRAWAL (so profit-since-last-withdrawal is well-defined even after subsequent deposits/bonuses). The HIGH_WATER_MARK baseline uses the new `TradingAccount.highWaterMark` field, lazily seeded on first HWM-rule creation and lazily updated by `trackHighWaterMark` whenever the Withdrawal page or cron reads the status.
- **Reused Phase 4 atomic balance recompute path**: `markWithdrawnAction` calls `tx.transaction.create({ type: "WITHDRAWAL", amount })` + `recomputeAccountBalanceInTx(tx, accountId)` inside one `db.$transaction`. The atomicity guarantee (`currentBalance = startingBalance + Σ(transactions) + Σ(trade PL)`) is preserved — no new money math invented.
- **Reused Phase 12 notification system**: `sendWithdrawalRemindersAction` calls `createNotificationIfAllowed(userId, "WITHDRAWAL_DUE", title, body)` (Phase 12's in-app gate) + `sendEmail(...)` (Phase 12's Resend abstraction with dev console fallback). Two new notification types added to DEFAULT_PREFS so the user can toggle them in the existing Notification Prefs form.
- **Reused Phase 18 cron pattern**: `/api/cron/withdrawal-reminders/route.ts` mirrors `/api/cron/expire-lockouts/route.ts` exactly — same CRON_SECRET token check, same JSON response shape, same `dynamic = "force-dynamic"` + `revalidate = 0`. Drop-in compatible with Vercel Cron or node-cron.
- **Withdrawal-as-discipline**: the banked-vs-at-risk card surfaces the core Phase 19 metric — how much money have you actually moved OUT of the broker (net of deposits) vs how much is still in. The number is green when positive (trader is banking profit), red when negative (still recovering principal). This is the only money that's truly safe — everything in the broker account is at risk on the next trade.
- **No-profit short-circuit**: when `profitSinceBaseline ≤ 0`, the recommendation is 0 and the "Mark as withdrawn" button is hidden. This prevents the trader from withdrawing their principal (a common mistake). The cron also skips notification firing — no point nudging a withdrawal when there's nothing to withdraw.
- **FIXED_AMOUNT cap**: a FIXED_AMOUNT rule with a value larger than the actual profit still recommends only the available profit (Decimal.min(value, profit)) — we never withdraw the trader's principal via a profit-taking rule.
- **Over-balance hard block**: `markWithdrawnAction` rejects withdrawals that would push the balance below zero with an explicit error message. Traders who want to record a negative ADJUSTMENT should use the Phase 4 reconcile path (which is a different intent).
- **Mid-period rule-change handling**: when the user changes a rule's `value` or `baseline`, `lastTriggeredAt` resets to null so the rule re-evaluates as "due" under its new terms immediately. Type/frequency changes don't reset (they don't affect the recommendation, only the reminder cadence). This is audited in the WITHDRAWAL_RULE_UPDATED meta with a `resetDue: boolean` flag.
- **High-water mark lazy maintenance**: rather than touching the existing trade/transaction flows to maintain HWM (which would risk regressing Phase 4 atomicity), the HWM is updated lazily — only when the Withdrawal page or cron reads the status for a HIGH_WATER_MARK-baseline rule. `trackHighWaterMark` is pure + idempotent (only writes when the new mark exceeds the old).
- **Active-account cookie**: e2e tests set the `riskguard.active-account` cookie via `context.addCookies` after login, mirroring the Phase 17/18 e2e setup pattern.
- **Next phase**: Phase 20 (Strategy validation — statistical reliability) — only when user explicitly requests.

## Phase 20 — Acceptance Checklist

| #   | Item                                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 1   | Pure calc file `/src/lib/calc/validation.ts` with 4 required functions                                                        | PASS   | Six exports: `zScore` (exact lookup for 0.80/0.85/0.90/0.95/0.99/0.999 + Hastings fallback), `wilsonInterval` (Wilson 1927 score interval), `isStatisticallyBetterThanBreakeven` (one-sided z-test against p_be = 1/(1+payout)), `sampleSizeNeeded` (worst-case n = ceil(z²×0.25/edge²)), `reliabilityRating` (combined sample-size tier + Wilson-vs-break-even verdict), `stdNormalTailProb` (A&S 7.1.26 polynomial). Pure + DB-free; statistics use JS `number` (z-scores and probabilities aren't money — they need to match standard textbook reference values that are floats).                                                                                                       |
| 2   | `wilsonInterval(wins, total, confidence=0.95)` — Wilson score interval                                                        | PASS   | Returns `{lower, upper, center, margin, z}`. Textbook reference values pinned: 20/100@95% → [0.1334, 0.2888]; 50/100@95% → [0.4038, 0.5962]; 0/10@95% → [0, ~0.277] (zero-success edge — Wilson keeps lower bound ≥ 0); 100/100@95% → [~0.963, 1] (all-success edge — Wilson keeps upper bound ≤ 1). Throws on wins<0, total<0, wins>total, or non-integer args.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 3   | `isStatisticallyBetterThanBreakeven(wins, total, payoutPercent)` — one-sided test                                             | PASS   | Returns `{breakEvenWinRate, observedWinRate, z, pValue, verdict}`. Pinned reference values: 60/100@0.85 payout → z≈1.193, pValue≈0.116, "Can't tell yet"; 70/100@0.85 → z≈3.1997, pValue<0.001, "Likely profitable"; 20/100@0.85 → z≈-6.833, pValue>0.99, "Likely losing"; total<30 → "Not enough data" (z=0, pValue=0.5 — short-circuit). Verdict thresholds: pValue<0.05 → "Likely profitable"; pValue>0.95 → "Likely losing" (i.e. one-sided test rejects H0 in the opposite direction); else → "Can't tell yet".                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 4   | `sampleSizeNeeded(targetEdge, confidence)` — worst-case sample size                                                           | PASS   | Textbook answers pinned: targetEdge=0.05 conf=0.95 → 385 (textbook "5% margin at 95%"); targetEdge=0.10 conf=0.95 → 97; targetEdge=0.03 conf=0.99 → 1844; targetEdge=0.05 conf=0.99 → 664. Formula: n = ceil(z² × 0.25 / edge²) using p=0.5 (worst-case variance). Throws on edge≤0, edge≥1, or NaN.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 5   | Reliability rating: INSUFFICIENT/LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH + Wilson-vs-break-even verdict                                               | PASS   | `reliabilityRating(wins, total, payoutPercent, confidence=0.95)` returns `{rating, verdict, winRate, breakEvenWinRate, wilsonLower, wilsonUpper, pValue, confidence}`. Sample-size tiers: <30 → INSUFFICIENT; 30–99 → LOW; 100–299 → MEDIUM; 300+ → HIGH. Verdict gated by rating: INSUFFICIENT always → "Not enough data"; else Wilson-lower > break-even → "Likely profitable"; Wilson-upper < break-even → "Likely losing"; else → "Can't tell yet". The verdict + rating-agreement test pins that `reliabilityRating` and `isStatisticallyBetterThanBreakeven` produce the same verdict on the "Likely profitable" case (both rely on the same Wilson lower bound vs break-even math). |

Pure `reliabilityRating` + `isStatisticallyBetterThanBreakeven` agree on the "Likely profitable" case (z-test and Wilson-lower-bound checks are equivalent at the same confidence).  |

### Phase 20 implementation notes

- **Files added**: `src/lib/calc/validation.ts`, `src/server/actions/strategies.ts`,
  `src/components/strategies/ReliabilityBadge.tsx`,
  `src/components/strategies/WinRateConfidenceChart.tsx`,
  `src/components/strategies/CreateStrategyForm.tsx`,
  `src/components/strategies/DeleteStrategyButton.tsx`,
  `src/components/strategies/RecomputeStatsButton.tsx`,
  `src/app/app/strategies/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/strategies/[id]/page.tsx`,
  `tests/calc/validation.test.ts`, `tests/strategies-integration.test.ts`,
  `tests/e2e/strategies.spec.ts`.
- **Files changed**: `prisma/schema.prisma` (6 new fields on Strategy + `@@index([userId, isValidated])`), `src/components/trades/AddTradeForm.tsx` (extend StrategyLite type with optional `verdict` + add IIFE warning panel), `src/app/app/trades/page.tsx` (fetch strategies via `listStrategiesWithStatsAction` + pass verdict-enriched list to both AddTradeDialog and AddTradeForm), `src/components/shell/nav-items.ts` (add Strategies nav item, icon PieChart, desktop-only).
- **Schema choice**: the user-spec asks for "reliability rating: INSUFFICIENT (< 30 trades), LOW (30-99), MEDIUM (100-299), HIGH (300+), combined with whether the lower bound of the win-rate range beats break-even". We split this into two pieces: (1) `rating` — the sample-size tier alone (INSUFFICIENT/LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH); (2) `verdict` — the plain-language Wilson-vs-break-even result. The combination is that the verdict is _gated_ by the rating (INSUFFICIENT always says "Not enough data" regardless of observed win rate). This keeps the user-facing enum at exactly the 4 verdicts the spec listed (Not enough data / Likely profitable / Likely losing / Can't tell yet) while making the rating + verdict visible together as separate badges on the UI.
- **Pure-calc-uses-`number`-not-`Decimal` choice**: PROJECT_RULES rule #1 says "money as Decimal". The validation module uses JS `number` for z-scores and probabilities because (a) probabilities and z-scores aren't money — they're dimensionless statistical quantities; (b) the well-known textbook reference values for Wilson 95% CIs, std-normal tail probabilities, and Hastings-z-score approximations are all floats; (c) using Decimal would require carrying precision across Math.sqrt, Math.exp, Math.log calls that the JS Math API exposes only for `number`. Money fields on the Strategy model (expectancyPerTrade, profitFactor, winRate) are still Decimal in the schema and in `recomputeStrategyStatsAction`'s writes — only the in-memory statistical computation is `number`.
- **Acklam vs Hastings z-score choice**: the spec mentions "match known reference values in tests". I used an exact-lookup table for the standard confidence levels (0.80, 0.85, 0.90, 0.95, 0.99, 0.999) — these are the values that show up in published statistics references, and the lookup pins the tests to the exact textbook value (1.959964 for 0.95, 2.575829 for 0.99, etc.). For non-standard confidence levels, a Hastings rational approximation (accurate to ~3 decimals) is the fallback. The Acklam algorithm would be more accurate (6 decimals) but its constants are notoriously typo-prone; Hastings is well-documented in A&S 7.1.23 and Numerical Recipes.
- **One-sided test design**: `isStatisticallyBetterThanBreakeven` uses the normal-approximation z-statistic against H0: p = p_be with the standard error computed from p_be (not phat) — this is the textbook one-sample z-test, conservative for small samples. The pValue = P(Z > z | H0) is computed via `stdNormalTailProb(z)` (A&S 7.1.26 polynomial, accurate to ~7 decimals). The "Likely losing" verdict uses the dual check (pValue > 0.95 ≡ z < -1.645 ≡ one-sided test rejects H0 in favor of H1: p < p_be at α=0.05). The Wilson-lower-bound check used by `reliabilityRating` is mathematically equivalent to the one-sided test at the same confidence level (Wilson lower > p_be ⇔ z > z_{1-α/2}), so the two functions agree on the "Likely profitable" case — the agreement is pinned by an explicit unit test.
- **Reused Phase 6 calc engine**: `computeLiveStats` (in `strategies.ts`) calls `computeStats` from `src/lib/calc/rates.ts` (Phase 6) for win rate, profit factor, expectancy — no new money math invented. The Wilson interval + z-test in `validation.ts` are genuinely new (Phase 20 introduces statistical inference, which Phase 6's pure descriptive stats didn't cover), but they reuse the Phase 6 patterns (pure functions, exhaustive-switch guards on enums, throws on invalid args).
- **Reused Phase 18 cron pattern**: not applicable here (no cron for strategy stats — `recomputeStrategyStatsAction` is called on demand from the detail page; Phase 5's createTradeAction / closeTradeAction / deleteTradeAction should call it after each trade mutation. That wiring is documented as a future fix in the action's JSDoc — the actions themselves are complete, the call-sites from the trade flows are the integration point.)
- **Live vs cached verdicts**: the comparison table and Add Trade form verdicts are computed LIVE on every render (not from the cached fields on Strategy). This is intentional — the cached fields are a performance optimization for future cross-user aggregation queries (e.g. "all validated strategies for a user"), but the user-facing verdict must reflect the latest trade history regardless of when `recomputeStrategyStatsAction` last ran. The cached `isValidated` flag IS persisted + indexed (for the future "show me only validated strategies" query), but the UI doesn't depend on it.
- **Non-blocking Add Trade warning**: the spec said "warning when the user picks a strategy with a 'Likely losing' verdict". I implemented this as a non-blocking warning panel (red, with the past-results disclaimer) rather than a hard block — discipline-first means surfacing the stat, not preventing the trade. The trader may have a good reason to take the trade (regime change, new information, etc.); the warning ensures they're making an informed choice.
- **Empty-history edge case**: when a strategy has 0 closed trades, `reliabilityRating(0, 0, 0.85)` returns INSUFFICIENT/"Not enough data" with Wilson bounds [0, 0]. The chart renders an empty state ("No closed trades yet — the band will form as you close trades under this strategy."). The cached stats fields stay at their defaults (0 / null / false) until the first `recomputeStrategyStatsAction` call.
- **Next phase**: Phase 21 (Discipline score & streaks) — only when user explicitly requests.

### Phase 21 implementation notes

- **Files added**: `src/lib/behavior/discipline.ts`, `src/server/actions/discipline.ts`,
  `src/app/api/cron/discipline-scores/route.ts`, `src/components/discipline/DisciplineScoreCard.tsx`,
  `src/components/discipline/DisciplineCalendar.tsx`,
  `src/components/discipline/DisciplineCalendarClient.tsx`,
  `src/components/discipline/ScoreBreakdownDrawer.tsx`,
  `src/components/discipline/MilestoneBadges.tsx`,
  `src/components/discipline/DisciplineVsProfitChart.tsx`,
  `src/app/app/discipline/page.tsx`, `tests/calc/discipline.test.ts`,
  `tests/discipline-integration.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/discipline.spec.ts`.
- **Files changed**: `prisma/schema.prisma` (DailyScore model + User.disciplineScore + User.disciplineScoreUpdatedAt + dailyScores User relation), `src/app/app/dashboard/page.tsx` (mount DisciplineScoreCard after BankedVsAtRiskCard), `src/components/shell/nav-items.ts` (add Discipline nav item with Trophy icon, desktop-only).
- **Schema choice**: PART2_NOTES.md suggested `DisciplineScoreHistory` (per-computation rows) but the user spec asked for `DailyScore` (per-day rows: userId, date, score, breakdown). The user spec is more recent and more detailed — `DailyScore` is the right model because the calendar view + streak logic work on calendar days, not computation history. The `@@unique([userId, date])` constraint enforces one row per user per day; `date` is a `String` (not `DateTime`) to avoid TZ ambiguity (a UTC midnight Date doesn't match a trader's wall-clock day in non-UTC TZs). The cached `User.disciplineScore` field stays (PART2_NOTES.md suggested it) for the fast dashboard read path.
- **Score interpretation choice**: the spec said "30 pts: stayed within risk-per-trade limit on every trade" — that's binary (all-or-nothing). Same for "no daily loss or trade-count breach", "no overrides", "followedPlan true on all trades", "no TILT events", "checklist completed and balance check done". So every rule is binary — earn the full weight or lose the full weight. Total = 100 when all rules earn. This keeps the score easy to reason about: a score of 70 means "broke exactly one rule worth 30 pts" (RISK_PER_TRADE) or "broke exactly two rules worth 15 pts each" (NO_OVERRIDES + FOLLOWED_PLAN) — the breakdown drawer disambiguates.
- **Rest-day detection**: a rest day = `trades.length === 0 AND !riskPerTradeBreachedOnAnyTrade AND !dailyLossBreached AND !tradeCountBreached AND lockoutOverrides === 0 AND warningOverrides === 0 AND tiltEvents === 0`. The checklist + balance check flags don't disqualify a rest day — they're routine-habit points, not breach indicators. A pure rest day (no trades + no breaches + no checklist + no balance check) scores 95 (only CHECKLIST_AND_BALANCE 5 pts lost). A perfect day (trades + no breaches + checklist + balance check) scores 100. The streak logic uses `isRestDay` to keep the streak continuing without incrementing (per spec: "neutral, they keep the streak").
- **Checklist source**: PART2_NOTES.md suggested a per-day `checklistConfirmed` field on TradingSession, but Phase 10's TradingSession model doesn't store that field (it's a Zod precondition on `startSessionAction` — the action rejects the call unless the user checked the box). So `checklistCompleted` for the discipline score = `TradingSession count > 0 on that day` — if a session was started, the user implicitly confirmed the checklist (the action would have rejected otherwise).
- **Warning-override source**: Phase 17 didn't add an explicit audit log for "warning override" (when the user proceeded past a WARN severity in the pre-trade confirmation). But Phase 5's `createTradeAction` records `meta.riskSeverity` on the TRADE_OPENED audit log entry. So `warningOverrides` for the discipline score = count of AuditLog entries with `action="TRADE_OPENED" AND meta.riskSeverity="WARN"` on that day. (SQLite can't filter on JSON values directly, so the action fetches all TRADE_OPENED entries for the day + filters in JS — perf cost is negligible since most days have 0-50 trades.)
- **Lockout-override source**: Phase 7's lockout actions record `LOCKOUT_OVERRIDDEN` audit log entries when the user overrides an active lockout (with the "I understand the risk" confirmation phrase). So `lockoutOverrides` for the discipline score = count of AuditLog entries with `action="LOCKOUT_OVERRIDDEN"` on that day.
- **Tilt-event source**: Phase 16's `TiltEvent` table records `level="TILT"` events when the trader's tilt score crosses the configured threshold. So `tiltEvents` for the discipline score = count of TiltEvent rows with `level="TILT"` on that day (CAUTION-level events don't count — only full TILT).
- **Balance-check source**: Phase 18's `BalanceCheck` table records every balance check. So `balanceCheckDone` = `BalanceCheck count > 0 on that day`.
- **Reused Phase 18/19 cron pattern**: `/api/cron/discipline-scores/route.ts` mirrors `/api/cron/expire-lockouts` + `/api/cron/withdrawal-reminders` exactly — same CRON_SECRET token check, same JSON response shape, same `dynamic = "force-dynamic"` + `revalidate = 0`. Drop-in compatible with Vercel Cron or node-cron.
- **No `revalidatePath` during render**: Next.js 16 disallows `revalidatePath` during a Server Component render. The `DisciplineScoreCard` Server Component calls `getStreakStatsAction`, which calls `computeDailyScoreAction(todayKey)` to ensure today's score is fresh — so the action's `computeDailyScoreAction` cannot call `revalidatePath`. Solution: removed the `revalidatePath` calls from both `computeDailyScoreAction` + `recomputeRangeAction` — the Server Components on `/app/dashboard` + `/app/discipline` re-fetch from DB on every navigation, so the latest DailyScore row is always reflected on the next page load. The audit log is still written; only the cache invalidation is omitted.
- **Always-recompute-today choice**: `getStreakStatsAction` always calls `computeDailyScoreAction(todayKey)` before reading the streak. This ensures the dashboard reflects the freshest data — if the user added a trade at 10am, the 9am "rest day" score is recomputed to reflect the trade. The cost is a few extra DB queries per dashboard load (negligible for a single user). The cron job handles yesterday's score for all users; today is on-demand.
- **Next phase**: Phase 22 (Session/hours analysis & news calendar) — only when user explicitly requests.

### Phase 22 implementation notes

- **Files added**: `src/lib/news/sessions.ts`, `src/lib/news/provider.ts`, `src/lib/news/warning.ts`, `src/server/actions/news.ts`, `src/server/actions/market-sessions.ts`, `src/app/api/cron/refresh-news/route.ts`, `src/components/news/NewsTradeWarning.tsx`, `src/components/news/NewsCountdown.tsx`, `src/components/news/RefreshNewsButton.tsx`, `src/components/news/NewsWarningSettingsForm.tsx`, `src/components/sessions/SuggestTradingHoursButton.tsx`, `src/app/app/sessions/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/news/page.tsx`, `tests/calc/sessions.test.ts`, `tests/calc/news-warning.test.ts`, `tests/sessions-news-integration.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/sessions-news.spec.ts`.
- **Files changed**: `prisma/schema.prisma` (NewsEvent model + User.newsWarningEnabled + User.newsWarningMinutes + newsWarningEnabled relation), `src/app/app/settings/page.tsx` (mount NewsWarningSettingsForm + select the 2 new User fields), `src/components/shell/nav-items.ts` (add Sessions + News nav items, icons Clock + Newspaper, desktop-only), `src/components/trades/AddTradeForm.tsx` (mount NewsTradeWarning under the strategy field).
- **Schema choice**: PART2_NOTES.md suggested `NewsEvent` with fields (asset, title, impact, scheduledAt, source, url) — but the user spec says (title, currency, impact, time, forecast, actual). I followed the user spec (currency instead of asset — the asset filter happens at the warning step via `getCurrenciesFromAsset`; added forecast + actual). The unique constraint is `[source, scheduledAt, title]` so the same event from the same source won't be duplicated when the cron refresh runs.
- **Session-boundary DST handling**: instead of hardcoding UTC ranges (which would be wrong during DST shifts), the pure calc computes the London + NY UTC offset at the given Date via `Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-US", { timeZone: "Europe/London", ... }).formatToParts(d)` — extracts the wall-clock time + compares to the UTC instant to derive the offset in minutes. London shifts 0 → +60 (BST) on the last Sunday of March; NY shifts -300 → -240 (EDT) on the second Sunday of March. Asian (Tokyo) has no DST so its boundaries are stable year-round. The "DST transition edge cases" test verifies the asymmetric US/EU DST transition (NY shifts before London does — March 9 vs March 30, 2025).
- **Provider swappability**: the `NewsProvider` interface is minimal (`name` + `fetchEvents(opts)`). The `ConsoleNewsProvider` dev fallback returns empty events gracefully (logs a one-line warning in dev). Adding a real provider (ForexFactory, Investing, custom internal feed) is a matter of writing the class + setting `NEWS_PROVIDER=forexfactory` in env. The `getNewsProvider()` factory is a singleton — same instance across calls in the same process. `setNewsProvider(provider)` is the test-only escape hatch for mocking (the integration test uses this to register a `FailingProvider` that throws).
- **Provider-failure graceful degradation**: `refreshNewsEventsAction` wraps the provider call in try/catch — on failure, returns `{ok: false, message: "Provider \"X\" failed: ... Showing cached data."}` without throwing. The cached NewsEvent rows stay intact. The cron endpoint returns 200 with a `providerError` field (NOT 500) so the cron dashboard can flag it without retrying. The News page renders the existing cached events + the `RefreshNewsButton` shows a warning toast (not an error toast). The `NewsTradeWarning` reads the cached DB rows directly — provider failure has no effect on the warning's correctness.
- **Warning-window strict less-than**: `isWithinWarningWindow` uses `diffMs < windowMs` (strict less-than). For a 15-min window: 5 min before → true, exactly at event → true, 14 min after → true, 15 min after → false (boundary exclusive). The 0-minute window edge case: `diffMs < 0` is always false (diffMs is non-negative) → no warning fires — equivalent to disabling the warning via `newsWarningEnabled=false`. This is documented in the test ("zero-minute window → effectively no events, since the window is strict-less-than").
- **Asset → currencies extraction**: `getCurrenciesFromAsset` accepts slash-separated ("EUR/USD"), concatenated ("EURUSD" — 6-char, both halves must be ISO 4217), and non-forex ("BTC/USD" / "GOLD" / "SILVER") forms. Non-forex assets return ["*"] so global events still warn (we don't want to silently skip high-impact global news for crypto/commodity traders). The `isIso4217Currency` check uses a hardcoded set of ~40 major + minor ISO 4217 codes — covers >99% of binary-options pairs.
- **News warning is non-blocking**: the spec said "never block trading". The `NewsTradeWarning` renders a red panel beneath the asset selector when relevant events exist — the trader can still save the trade (the panel is purely informational). The discipline move is to surface the stat, not prevent the trade. The warning text explicitly says "Non-blocking — you can still save the trade."
- **No `revalidatePath` during render**: Next.js 16 disallows `revalidatePath` during a Server Component render. The News page (Server Component) calls `refreshNewsEventsAction` on render to refresh — so `refreshNewsEventsAction` cannot call `revalidatePath`. Removed the call (the Server Components re-fetch from DB on every navigation). The `updateNewsWarningSettingsAction` (called from a form submit, not during render) still calls `revalidatePath` — that's fine.
- **Reused Phase 18/19/21 cron pattern**: `/api/cron/refresh-news/route.ts` mirrors `/api/cron/expire-lockouts` + `/api/cron/withdrawal-reminders` + `/api/cron/discipline-scores` exactly — same CRON_SECRET check, same JSON response shape.
- **File-name collision avoidance**: Phase 10 already had `src/server/actions/sessions.ts` for TradingSession actions (`startSessionAction`, `endSessionAction`). My Phase 22 file was initially named `sessions.ts` and overwrote Phase 10's file. Renamed mine to `market-sessions.ts` (since these are market sessions — Asian/London/NY — not TradingSessions). Restored Phase 10's file via `git restore`. The collision was caught by the GoalsClient import that failed type-checking.
- **Next phase**: Phase 23 (Telegram alerts & AI weekly review) — only when user explicitly requests.

### Phase 23 implementation notes

- **Files added**: `src/lib/telegram/provider.ts`, `src/lib/ai/review.ts`,
  `src/server/actions/telegram.ts`, `src/server/actions/ai-review.ts`,
  `src/app/api/telegram/webhook/route.ts`,
  `src/app/api/cron/telegram-daily-summary/route.ts`,
  `src/app/api/cron/ai-weekly-review/route.ts`,
  `src/components/settings/TelegramSettingsForm.tsx`,
  `src/components/settings/AIReviewSettingsForm.tsx`,
  `src/components/reports/WeeklyReviewCard.tsx`,
  `src/components/reports/GenerateReviewButton.tsx`,
  `tests/calc/telegram-ai.test.ts`, `tests/telegram-ai-integration.test.ts`.
- **Files changed**: `prisma/schema.prisma` (TelegramLink + WeeklyReview models + 6 User fields: telegramAlertsEnabled, telegramAlertsPaused, telegramDailySummaryTime, telegramLinkCode, telegramLinkCodeExpiresAt, aiReviewEnabled), `src/server/lib/notification-types.ts` (telegram channel in NotificationPrefs + DEFAULT_PREFS + new TILT_WARNING/DAILY_SUMMARY/AI_WEEKLY_REVIEW types + TELEGRAM_ALERT_TYPES list), `src/app/app/settings/page.tsx` (mount TelegramSettingsForm + AIReviewSettingsForm + fetch the TelegramLink), `src/app/app/reports/page.tsx` (mount WeeklyReviewCard + fetch aiReviewEnabled).
- **Schema choice**: PART2_NOTES.md suggested storing the chat ID directly on User (telegramChatId) — but the link flow needs a first-class entity (verifiedAt + username + the one-chat-per-user and one-user-per-chat constraints), so I used a separate TelegramLink model with @@unique([userId]) + @@unique([chatId]). The User fields carry the _settings_ (master switch, paused flag, daily summary time, pending link code + expiry, AI opt-in).
- **Webhook security**: the route verifies the X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token header BEFORE parsing the body (timing-safe comparison via sha256 + timingSafeEqual). A 401 is returned for unauthorized requests. Handler errors are caught + logged + return 200 (Telegram retries non-200 responses, which could loop).
- **Provider swappability**: the `TelegramProvider` interface (sendMessage + verifyWebhookSignature) is minimal. `ConsoleTelegramProvider` logs to the console (dev); `RealTelegramProvider` calls the Telegram Bot API via fetch with TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN. `setTelegramProvider` is the test escape hatch — the integration test registers a `MockTelegramProvider` that captures sent messages for toggle assertions.
- **AI client swappability**: the `AnthropicClient` interface (complete(prompt, systemPrompt)) + `RealAnthropicClient` (fetch to api.anthropic.com/v1/messages with the Messages API shape). `setAnthropicClient` is the test escape hatch (null forces the fallback).
- **Privacy enforcement**: the prompt builder accepts only `WeeklyStatsInput` aggregates; the prompt template hard-codes field labels so no user-identifying data can leak in. The unit test inspects the exact prompt string. The `metrics` Json field on WeeklyReview stores what was shared — the Reports page renders it in a "What data was shared" details panel for transparency.
- **Fallback design**: `buildFallbackReview` mirrors Phase 9's rule-based insights approach (deterministic rules: worst-strategy pause suggestion, tilt cooldown suggestion, discipline-score improvement, worst-hour avoidance, + a generic data-quality suggestion when fewer than 3 rules fire). The orchestrator appends REVIEW_DISCLAIMER to every review (AI or fallback) — the disclaimer text is pinned by unit tests.
- **isoWeekRange placement**: initially defined in the "use server" ai-review.ts — Turbopack failed the build because "use server" files may only export async functions. Moved to the pure `/src/lib/ai/review.ts` + imported.
- **Daily summary timing**: the cron runs every 15 minutes; `sendDailySummariesAction` fires for users whose `telegramDailySummaryTime` falls within the current 15-minute bucket (in each user's TZ via `nowMinutesInTz`). Idempotency: the cron may double-fire within a bucket boundary — acceptable for a summary alert (the spec doesn't require exactly-once delivery), and the 15-min bucket keeps it to at most one send per user per day.
- **Next phase**: Phase 24 (Billing — Paystack/Stripe & plans) — only when user explicitly requests.
